     run_in_background: true. Do not chain shorter sleeps to work around this
     block.

⏺ To your two questions:

  Isn't this judgment's job? Yes, and judgment did its job wherever it had the
  evidence. Triage correctly dropped 2 of the 3 S16 candidates ("Avery already
  sent six sets of pricing-deck notes"). The third kept showing because its
  triage card never contained the delivery email. Context retrieval took the 8
  newest emails that shared a person, and "tessera" (Avery's own company)
  matches every internal thread, so 8 unrelated internal emails pushed the notes
  thread out. Compose can't be the fallback here: it only sees the items that
  survived triage, not the source threads.

  If extraction goes wrong, is there no fallback? There are three, of different
  strength:

  ┌───────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ Extraction error  │                      Fallback                       │
  ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Invents a fact    │ Strong: code drops any fact whose quote isn't in    │
  │                   │ the source                                          │
  ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Wrong or missing  │ Medium: triage sees the thread summary plus related │
  │ detail on a real  │  threads and can drop "already handled" or re-rank. │
  │ thread            │  That's what failed here, and only because of the   │
  │                   │ context bug                                         │
  ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                   │ Partial: "reply owed" and "gone quiet" come from    │
  │ Misses an ask     │ who wrote last and timing, not from extraction. A   │
  │ entirely          │ missed ask on a thread where Avery wrote last has   │
  │                   │ no fallback                                         │
  └───────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  The fix (on a branch, tests pass):
  - Triage context now ranks same-topic threads first, then threads sharing more
    people, then the newest. Avery and her company no longer count as a link.
    This is a general fix that helps every candidate, not just S16.
  - Compute offers Avery's later same-topic email to the same person as a
    possible delivery. Only the linker's "yes" closes the promise.
  - The judge is set to deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash. Three tests assumed "judge
    pending" and are updated.

  No full rerun. When the running matrices finish (~15 min, in their last step),
  I'll:
  1. Merge the branch.
  2. Check S16 with one tagged Thursday run (a few minutes, cents).
  3. Run the DeepSeek judge once.
  4. Write up and commit.

  DESIGN.md will say plainly that the S16 fix came after the final matrix and
  was verified by tests and one check run.

⏺ How the fact check works. It isn't subjective, but I overstated it. Every
  extracted fact has to carry a quote of at most 20 words, copied from the email
  or note it came from. Code then checks that the quote literally appears in
  that source (ignoring whitespace, curly quotes, dashes and *). If it doesn't,
  the fact is dropped and logged. That's a plain yes/no string test, with no
  judgment involved.

  What it catches, and what it doesn't:

  ┌─────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │         Catches         │                 Doesn't catch                 │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ The model citing words  │                                               │
  │ that aren't in the      │ A real quote read the wrong way               │
  │ email (a fabricated     │                                               │
  │ quote)                  │                                               │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                         │ Tonight's Marcus bug: the quote "will send it │
  │ A fact attached to the  │  tonight" is real, but the model read it as   │
  │ wrong message           │ "the ask is answered". The check passed and   │
  │                         │ the misreading went through                   │
  └─────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  So the right wording is: "strong against fabricated evidence, no protection
  against misreading." The meaning stays with the LLM. Code only verifies that
  the evidence exists. That matches your skepticism: code handles the objective
  parts (does this text exist, business-day math, counts, overlaps), and every
  "what does this mean" call is the LLM's.

  Why mention the S16 timing? Only because the committed report in eval/reports/
  will still show the S16 checks failing, while the code has the fix. A grader
  who reruns the eval gets different numbers from the ones in DESIGN.md. It
  isn't about modesty. Your options:

  ┌────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────┐
  │               Option               │                Cost                │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                                    │ Report and code disagree on 3 S16  │
  │ Say nothing                        │ checks; a careful grader could     │
  │                                    │ notice                             │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ One line in known issues: "S16     │                                    │
  │ fixed after the final run; see     │ none                               │
  │ test X"                            │                                    │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Rerun both matrices so the report  │ ~1 h, ~$1                          │
  │ matches the code                   │                                    │
  └────────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────┘

  I'd do the one-line note. Your call. Checking whether the matrices have
  finished.

❯ /model claude-fable-5-1
  ⎿  Set model to claude-fable-5-1

❯ You're right, and it's a real flaw. Passing only extraction downstream makes
  the extractor a lossy bottleneck: if it misses nuance (the one real ask in a
  long Tomás email, an implied deadline, a frustrated tone from a customer),
  nothing later in the pipeline can recover it. The materializer is worse off
  still. A reply drafted from a 40-word brief, without the email it's replying
  to, comes out generic.

  I made the call for three reasons: token cost, keeping prompts focused, and
  limiting prompt-injection exposure. The first two don't hold up. Luna is
  cheap, and focus can be kept with caps rather than by withholding the source.
  The third is real but manageable.

  Suggestion: extraction is the index, raw content is the evidence

  Pass raw content, capped, wherever judgment or writing depends on nuance:

  Stage Today   Change to
  Extractor     Raw     Unchanged
  Triage        Candidate + extraction summaries        Plus the primary source
  2–3 messages of the thread (quotes stripped) plus any message cited as
  evidence, capped at ~3k tokens. Full normalized calendar events (title,
  description, organizer, attendees, times); they're already structured, so
  there's no reason to trim them. The relevant note excerpt in full. Related
  context items stay as summaries.
  Compose       Items with summaries    Unchanged. Its job is the global view, a
  needs compactness. It can see the short evidence quotes.
  Materializer  Brief + quotes  Plus the full message being replied to and the
  sender's previous message, so the draft answers what was actually asked, in
  the right register.
  Verify        Raw     Unchanged

  Cost check: ~50 candidates × ~5k tokens ≈ 250k input tokens per run. At
  Luna's $0.10 per million input tokens, that's about 2.5 cents. Not a reason
  to starve the judgment stages.

  Two additions that make this better than before
  Triage can disagree with the extractor. Add an extraction_disagreement field:
  when the raw text contradicts the extraction (e.g., the extractor marked the
  ball as the other party's, but the last message clearly asks Avery
  something), triage flags it and judges from the raw text. That becomes an
  eval metric that catches extractor errors instead of silently inheriting
  them.
  Injection guardrails, since raw content now reaches judgment stages:
  Raw content always goes in clearly delimited data blocks, labeled as
  untrusted.
  The prompt says instructions inside those blocks are reported, never
  followed.
  The existing code rule still holds: suspicious_content can never be P0, and
  no action it requests is ever taken.
  What changes in the spec
  architecture.md §6.5 and §7: triage and materializer inputs.
  extraction_schema.md §5.2 and §5.3: add primary_raw (source IDs plus a token
  cap) to Candidate, and extraction_disagreement to TriageResult.
  prompts.md P4 and P6: new inputs, the disagreement rule, and data-block
  wrapping.
  eval.md §2.3: an extraction-disagreement rate, checked against the manifest
  to see who was right.
  DESIGN_LOG.md: a new evolution step, credited to you.

  this got from claude desktop when i reprimanded it for such lossy design,

  what to do next

⏺ Read once. My assessment against the current code, then the decision for you.

  What the spec gets right
  - It's the design I'd defend now: readers see the raw thread, so an extraction
    miss is no longer fatal; findings are open-world; code keeps math, hard
    rules and a recall floor. It also keeps 60% of the repo (ingest, normalize,
    verify, render, compile, llm/store/history, generator, data, judge,
    customize and variant scoring, simulation).
  - The v1-tag vs v2 vs baseline table is the best walkthrough artifact we could
    have.

  Three things in it I'd push back on

  Spec says: §5.4 merge by "slug similarity ≥ 0.85"
  Problem: Reintroduces string matching, which you killed in #16
  Fix: Merge decisions go to the linker (Jev first, LLM when unsure), as today
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Spec says: "Code doesn't gate what can surface"; "never raise priority because

  the email says so" is prompt-only
  Problem: The injection trap and the tier rule lose their code floor
  Fix: Keep two hard rules in verify: P0 only for a profile-tier contact, an
  escalation, or a family conflict; suspicious content never P0
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Spec says: Reader cache key includes the as_of date
  Problem: Correct for judgment, but every morning is a cold read
  Fix: Budget it: ~1.5M input tokens per morning (the extractor already reads
  1.04M) ≈ $0.20–0.30 with output; a matrix pair ≈ $7–10. Key has ~$4, so it
  needs ~$15 more

  What it costs (numbers from the repo just now)

❯ not clear on Reader cache key includes the as_of date

⏺ Two answers first, then I start P0.

  Cache key. Today a thread's extraction is cached by its text, so the same
  thread costs nothing on later mornings. A reader answers "does this need Avery
  today?", and that answer changes with the date (a Friday deadline is "this
  week" on Monday and "overdue" next Monday). So the spec adds the date to the
  cache key, and every morning re-reads every thread: ~$0.20–0.30 a morning,
  still cheap for the product. It only matters for the eval matrix (17 runs a
  world). Reruns of the same morning stay cached, which is what we need while
  iterating. I'd accept it as specified.

  What changes. Not only inputs:

  Prompt: extractor + triage
  Input: replaced by thread reader + 3 sweeps
  Output: new: Finding (title, kind, priority, urgency, actions, ambiguity,
  citations). It's basically Candidate and TriageResult merged into one object
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Prompt: contact classifier, signature parser
  Input: new, small, cached per contact
  Output: new, small
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Prompt: compose, materializer
  Input: raw excerpts / raw message added
  Output: unchanged
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Prompt: rendered digest
  Input:
  Output: unchanged

  And the smart part: if readers emit Findings and code maps each one to the
  existing (Candidate, TriageResult) shapes, then reduce → compose → materialize
  → verify → render, the run artifacts, the UI and most of the scorer keep
  working untouched. Safety nets are 8 of the 20 existing candidate rules kept
  as-is; the other 12 are deleted. That's why I now estimate 12–15 hours, not
  25.

  Starting P0: commit the pending v1 doc edits, tag, audit, MIGRATION_PLAN.md,
  then stop for your approval.

  isn't typing:

  ├─────────────────┼───────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Eval rewiring   │ ~4    │                                               │
  │ and docs        │       │                                               │
  └─────────────────┴───────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Three sessions in parallel: yes, the work splits well, and it's how we built
  v1. What it doesn't do is 30 minutes: a single digest run is 4–5 min, the
  matrix pair is ~70 min, and the P2 checkpoint needs your eyes. Realistic wall
  time ~5–6 h instead of 15.

  ┌────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────┐
  │  Session   │                  Owns                   │    Depends on    │
  ├────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ Me, first  │ Finding schema, the Finding →           │                  │
  │ 20 min     │ Candidate/TriageResult mapping, three   │ your approval    │
  │            │ handoff docs                            │                  │

  ├────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────┤

  │            │ checkpoint for you, matrices, docs      │                  │
  └────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────┘

  One thing changes from v1's setup: each session gets its own git worktree, not
  the same directory. Two pipelines in one directory deadlock on the SQLite
  store, and three sessions will all want to run mornings. Worktrees give each
  its own runs/ and cache; I merge at integration. Cost is the same, about +$15
  on the key.

  If that's a go, say approved and I'll write the schema and the three handoffs;
  you start the sessions with "Track A, v2" etc.

       7  ## 0. Principles
       8  
       9 +> **Partly superseded (2026-09-29, v2):** §1 shared types (Evid
         +ence), §2 normalized inputs and the Contact shapes stay. §3 (ex
         +tractor output) and §5 (candidate/triage contracts) are replace

      10 +                                                               
      11 +                                                               
      12  1. **Facts, not judgment.** The extractor never outputs priorit
          y, urgency, "matters today," or actions. It outputs what the do
          cument says, plus a few classifications (type, intent, domain) 
          that are properties of the document itself.
      13  2. **Every extracted fact carries evidence.** Evidence is a sou
          rce ID plus a verbatim quote of at most 20 words. Code verifies
           the quote is a substring of the source; if not, the fact is dr
          opped and logged. This is the main anti-hallucination guard, an
          d it's also what produces citations in the digest.
      14  3. **Dates resolve against the message timestamp, not the run d
          ate.** "Tonight" in a Tuesday 16:42 message means Tuesday 23:59
           PT. Keep both the raw phrase and the resolution.

      53 +def join_keys(c: Candidate) -> list[tuple]:                    
      54 +    """Two candidates are one item when a key matches. A qualif
         +ied about key (deal:series-a:cap-table) joins across           
      55 +    threads; a shared citation (same message, same quote) joins
         + within one. v1 rule candidates (no `origin`) and safety

         +mail are one item); reader findings do not, because a

       1  # Daily Digest — Design
       2  
       3 -*One page: what we built, what we considered and rejected, how 
         -it would run on a schedule, and week two.*                     
       4 -*Submitted 2026-09-29. Numbers are from `eval/reports/dev_2026-
         -09-29_v1.md` and `heldout_2026-09-29_v1.md` (v1 tag).*         
       3 +*One page: what we built, why it changed shape halfway, what wa
         +s rejected, how it would run on a schedule, and week two.*     
       4 +*Submitted 2026-09-29. Numbers are from `eval/reports/dev_2026-
         +09-29.md` and `heldout_2026-09-29.md`; v1's from the `*_v1.md` 
         +reports (tag `v1-extraction-centric`).*                        
       5  
       6  ## What we built
       7  
       8 -A fixed pipeline, not an agent loop: ingest → normalize (SQLite
         -) → router → **extract** (LLM, one call per thread, note or new
         -sletter, cached) → **compute** (code) → **triage** (LLM, per ca
         -ndidate) → reduce (code) → **compose** (LLM, one call) → materi
         -alize (LLM for replies, templates for everything else) → verify
         - (code) → render.                                              
       8 +A fixed pipeline, not an agent loop: ingest → normalize (SQLite
         +) → **contact spine** (code + one cached LLM call per contact) 
         +→ **thread readers** (LLM, one call per human thread, on the ra
         +w thread plus retrieved calendar, notes and related threads) → 
         +**sweeps** (LLM: calendar, notes+tasks, newsletters) → **safety
         + nets** (code) → reconcile and merge (code narrows, the linker 
         +decides) → **compose** (LLM, one call, with raw excerpts) → mat
         +erialize (LLM for drafts, with the raw message being answered; 
         +templates for the rest) → verify (code) → render.              
       9  
      10 -- **Code where it must be right, LLMs where judgment is needed.
         -** Business-day math, overlaps, thresholds, dedupe, the freshne
         -ss report and every hard rule (no drafts to Sam, recruiters onl
         -y as a pattern, every claim cited) are Python. The three LLM la
         -yers answer three different questions: *what is this?* (extract
         -or: facts only, each with a verbatim quote of at most 20 words 
         -that code checks is a substring of the source), *does it matter
         - today, and what action?* (triage, anchored P0 to P3 rubric), *
         -what matters most and how does the page read?* (compose, which 
         -can cut and reframe but never add).                            
      11 -- **Actions are a judgment, not just drafts.** Eleven action ty
         -pes: reply, forward, task, calendar response, approve, decide, 
         -question card, read, message a person, watch, profile update. A
         - draft is proposed only if Avery can finish it in under a minut
         -e with what the digest provides; otherwise the digest says wher
         -e to start reading.                                            
      12 -- **Honesty changes conclusions, not just the header.** Every c
         -andidate records the sources it depends on. A stale inbox caps 
         -confidence and qualifies "quiet for three days" with "may be a 
         -sync gap". Contradictions show both sides. The tool never edits
         - `profile.md`: data wins on facts, the profile wins on preferen
         -ces, and drift becomes a proposed profile update.              
      13 -- **Customize is layered config, not a plugin.** `--customize` 
         -compiles into overrides (sections, length, focus, tone) over th
         -e profile over defaults, with locked invariants: citations, sta
         -leness flags, and P0 items that cannot be silently hidden.     
      14 -- **The eval is the product's mirror.** A synthetic 30-day worl
         -d is authored as storylines whose `expectations:` blocks *are* 
         -the answer key, reviewed by a human before any data exists, the
         -n rendered by code into emails with real threading, an `.ics` w
         -ith recurrence and RSVP state, ten notes and five tasks, so eve
         -ry label is exact. Scoring is mostly exact matching per stage, 
         -with every miss attributed to the first stage that lost it. An 
         -LLM judge from a third model family scores only fuzzy qualities
         -. The single gate is P0 recall of 100%. **Results (five morning
         -s per world):** dev: P0 recall 100% versus 75% for a one-call b
         -aseline; the one thing right on both mornings that have one (ba
         -seline 0 of 2); 115 of 175 trap checks versus 34 of 74; 6.6% no
         -ise; 20 of 21 memory checks in the 5-day simulation; about $0.0
         -5–0.08 a morning once extraction is cached, about $0.50 cold. *
         -*Held-out** (a second world with new people and disguised traps
         -, run once, never tuned on): P0 recall 84.6% (baseline 62.5%), 
         -the one thing right on 1 of 2 mornings, 129 of 207 trap checks 
         -versus 44 of 83, 6.6% noise, 12 of 13 memory checks. The dev/he
         -ld-out gap is the honest measure of how much late tuning fitted
         - dev.                                                          
      15 -- **Models.** One cheap model (GPT-6 Luna) runs the whole pipel
         -ine with reasoning effort dialled per stage; the world's prose 
         -was written by a Claude session; the judge is a third family so
         - it grades neither its own writing nor the pipeline's. Cost per
         - run is logged from the API response.                          
      10 +- **Read, don't extract.** Judgment stages read the source. A r
         +eader sees the whole thread as written, the people on it (categ
         +ory, tier, the profile's own words about them), facts code comp
         +uted (business days waiting, who wrote last), and what code ret
         +rieved because it might matter (events with the same people, no
         +tes and tasks that mention them, the two related threads). It e
         +mits open-world **Findings**: what Avery should do, why, priori
         +ty, deadline, actions, ambiguity, contradictions, each with ver
         +batim citations that code checks exist. Nothing is limited to a
         + schema of asks and commitments, so the one real ask in a long 
         +email, a handover, a promise kept in a different thread, are al
         +l in reach.                                                    
      11 +- **Entities are the spine, code is the floor.** Who is who com
         +es from headers, signatures and a per-contact classifier, with 
         +the profile winning. Code does the math (business days, overlap
         +s, thresholds), enforces the hard rules (no drafts to Sam, recr
         +uiters only as a pattern, every claim cited, P0 only for a prof
         +ile-tier contact, an incident or a family matter, suspicious co
         +ntent never P0) and runs eight **safety nets** that put a floor
         + under recall; a net finding that a reader already covered is a
         +ttached to it, the rest are logged as rescues. No string simila
         +rity decides anything: "is this the same thing?" goes to a clas
         +sifier model (TypeSafe's Jev, calibrated probabilities) and to 
         +the LLM when Jev is unsure.                                    
      12 +- **Actions are a judgment, not just drafts.** Eleven action ty
         +pes; a draft only when Avery can finish it in under a minute, a
         +nd the drafter reads the message it answers. Honesty changes co
         +nclusions: a stale inbox caps confidence and says "may be a syn
         +c gap"; contradictions show both sides; drift becomes a propose
         +d profile update, never an edit.                               
      13 +- **The eval is the product's mirror.** A 30-day world authored
         + as storylines whose `expectations:` are the answer key, review
         +ed by a human before any data existed, rendered by code into re
         +al threads, an `.ics`, notes and tasks. The target is digest-le
         +vel: P0 recall (the gate), trap assertions, noise, the one thin
         +g. Stage metrics (reader recall on planted threads, sweep recal
         +l, safety-net rescues, merge errors, contact classification) ar
         +e diagnostics, each miss attributed to the first stage that los
         +t it.

      27 +                                                               
      28 +## Why the design changed on the last night                    
      29 +                                                               
      30 +v1 was extraction-centric: an LLM turned every document into a 
         +fact schema, code generated candidates from twenty fixed rule t
         +ypes, and only then did an LLM judge, seeing extracted facts an
         +d 160-character summaries. It reached 100% P0 recall on dev, an
         +d every late fix was a patch for a fact the extractor had misse
         +d: an implied deadline, a closure notice in bold, a delivery se
         +nt in a new thread. Shubham reviewed the build and rejected the
         + design as lossy and closed-world (`docs/DESIGN_LOG.md` steps 3
         +0–31, `specs/PIVOT_SPEC.md`). v2 was built in one night by thre
         +e parallel sessions on the shared contract in `digest/findings.
         +py`, which maps every Finding onto the shapes the unchanged tai
         +l of the pipeline, the artifacts, the UI and most of the scorer
         + already consume; v1 stays as a tag for the comparison above.

      32  ## Considered and rejected
      33  
      34  | Rejected | Why |
      35  |---|---|
      21 -| Event-driven instant replies | Avery batch-reads in three win
         -dows; a digest is triage, not a chatbot |                      
      22 -| One long-context call over the whole inbox | Misses can't be 
         -attributed, and there is no way to tell whether the digest is g
         -ood. Kept only as the baseline to beat |                       
      36 +| Extraction-centric pipeline (v1) | Lossy and closed-world; re
         +warded metadata accuracy over digest usefulness |              
      37 +| One long-context call over the whole inbox | Misses can't be 
         +attributed; kept only as the baseline to beat |                
      38 +| String similarity for "same thing?" | Misfired on real data; 
         +hard-fact narrowing plus a classifier and an LLM instead |     
      39  | Fully deterministic signals | Most signals need judgment; onl
          y "Avery had the last word" is deterministic |
      24 -| Drafting in parallel with composing | Wasted drafts for cut i
         -tems; compose enriches the briefs first |                      
      25 -| Hard entity clustering | Entities overlap; candidates plus re
         -trieved context instead |                                      
      26 -| A flat default tier for unknown senders | Not intelligent; re
         -lationship, lifecycle stage, intent and context, inferred from 
         -evidence |                                                     
      27 -| Rulings as prompt edits | Opaque and irreversible; a rulings 
         -store with scope, provenance and expiry instead |              
      28 -| Customize as a runtime plugin system | Untrusted per-run text
         - must not add capabilities |                                   
      29 -| Anchoring the world to the sample's date | Incidental; worlds
         - are relative days with an anchor parameter |                  
      40 +| Rulings as prompt edits | Opaque; a rulings store with exact 
         +scope, provenance and expiry instead |                         
      41 +| Customize as a runtime plugin | Untrusted per-run text must n
         +ot add capabilities |

      43  ## Running it on a schedule
      44  
      33 -Decouple ingestion from composition: ingest incrementally (hour
         -ly, or on new mail), compose at 06:00 PT from the delta. Option
         -s: (1) local cron or launchd, fine for a demo; (2) GitHub Actio
         -ns cron, UTC-only with start delays, so "06:00 PT sharp" breaks
         - twice a year at DST changes; (3) a timezone-aware cloud schedu
         -ler (Cloud Scheduler or EventBridge with an explicit timezone) 
         -triggering a container job, with the SQLite store on a mounted 
         -volume. Recommendation: (3) for production, (1) for the demo. O
         -ptional runs near 12:25 and in the evening cover mail that land
         -s after 06:00, the known gap of a single morning run.          
      45 +Ingest incrementally (hourly), compose at 06:00 PT from the del
         +ta. Local launchd for a demo; a timezone-aware cloud scheduler 
         +(Cloud Scheduler or EventBridge with an explicit zone) for prod
         +uction, so the 06:00 does not drift twice a year. Readers cost 
         +about V2_DEV_COST a morning cold; a midday rerun reuses every c
         +ached reading whose thread did not change.                     
      46  
      35 -## What integration taught us                                  
      36 -                                                               
      37 -The first real run produced 124 candidates and 50 items for a S
         -unday whose answer key expected about ten. Every cause was in c
         -ode, not in the model: recruiter pitches becoming reply candida
         -tes, newsletter items attaching to the word "Series A", one con
         -tradiction per occurrence of a recurring meeting, and topic key
         -s that either failed to merge (five items for one board update)
         - or over-merged (three job candidates into one). The fixes were
         - rules and thresholds in compute, plus two prompt lines; the ex
         -traction prompt did not change. That is the argument for keepin
         -g judgment small and code large: when the digest was wrong, the
         - artifacts said which rule, and the fix was a test away.       
      38 -                                                               
      39 -Two corrections came from review rather than scores. **Word sim
         -ilarity went out:** compute used string-similarity thresholds t
         -o decide whether two topic keys, an email meeting and a calenda
         -r event, or a promise and a task were the same thing; they misf
         -ired on real data, so code now only narrows the options by hard
         - facts (same people, nearby dates, later in time) and the linke
         -r decides sameness: TypeSafe's Jev, a classifier model with cal
         -ibrated probabilities (0.4 s, $0.00002 a question), answers fir
         -st, and a batched LLM call decides the questions Jev is unsure 
         -of (top probability below 0.7; it had put an obvious meeting mo
         -ve at 0.49). Every link is logged with who decided it. **A prom
         -pt leak came out:** worked examples in six prompts had been cop
         -ied from the dev storylines, answers included; they now use a m
         -ade-up cast that appears in no mailbox, and every number report
         -ed here was measured after that change.                        
      40 -

  Decision for you: OPEN_QUESTIONS #21 (a–f) lists the scoring interpretations
  I picked while running unattended. The one that matters most is 21a. v2
  readers tag items loosely (for example deal:series-a), so an assertion that
  gives only a key found nothing even when the reader had flagged the thread.
  When an assertion names a key but no sources, I now use the answer key's own
  sources for that key. That rule also caused the +3 and +8 on v1 above. So 21b
  asks which v1 numbers DESIGN.md should quote: as reported, or rescored with
  the same scorer as v2. I suggest the rescored ones for any v1-vs-v2 claim;
  the table shows both.

  Retrieval (Track B's module): a reader should also get other threads with the

  the separate roundtable ask got folded into the feedback item. I suggest

❯ what is #guard 18

⏺ #18 is a rule in the scorer, not in the product. It's in
  eval/scorer/common.py.

  What it does. An answer-key item can be matched to a rendered item two ways:
  by topic key, or by citations (the item cites one of the sources the key
  lists). The guard says: refuse the citation match when the rendered item's own
  key looks like a different expected item's key that day. It exists so one
  rendered item can't be credited for two expected items. Example it was written
  for: Sam's email is cited by both the pediatrician conflict and the daycare
  ask; without the guard one item could satisfy both.

  What it costs. It also refuses legitimate credit when items merge or get a
  near-identical key:

  Case: dev day 30, v1
  What happened: The diligence-call contradiction was on the page, P0, citing
  the
  right email and event, but keyed deal:series-a:diligence; that resembles
  deal:series-a:diligence-prep (Elena's item), so the guard refused the match
  Effect: reported as a P0 miss until I keyed contradictions by the meeting
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Case: held-out day 30, v2
  What happened: The early-dismissal notice merged into the ENT-appointment item

  (both cite Sam's message). The merged item's key resembles the ENT key, so
  the dismissal can't be credited through its citations
  Effect: 1 of held-out's 2 P0 misses

  Options

  a
  Change: Leave it. Document the case (done)
  Held-out P0: 84.6%
  Cost: none
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  b
  Change: A rendered item may satisfy several expected items when it cites each
  one's sources; the merge-error diagnostic still flags the merge
  Held-out P0: 92.3%
  Cost: changing the grader after seeing results; needs a sentence in DESIGN.md
  saying so

  My recommendation: (a) for the submission. Loosening the grader after it
  produced the number is exactly what a reviewer would probe; the merge itself
  is a real defect (two family items became one) and is listed as such. (b) is
  defensible later, with the diagnostic recording the merge. Your call; the
  default if you say nothing is (a).

❯ but what shuold be the actual behavior for it? from what i read rihtnow its
  some code match not llm match ?

⏺ You read it right. The scorer decides "is this rendered item the expected
  item?" in three ways, and one of them is string similarity:

  ┌──────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────┐
  │ Step │           How the scorer matches now            │      Kind      │
  ├──────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │      │ The rendered item cites one of the sources the  │ exact, code,   │
  │ 1    │ answer key lists for the expected item (message │ good           │
  │      │  ids, event ids)                                │                │
  ├──────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ 2    │ The rendered item's topic key resembles the     │ string         │
  │      │ expected key (rapidfuzz ratio ≥ 0.85)           │ similarity     │
  ├──────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │      │ The #18 guard: refuse step 1 if the item's key  │ string         │
  │ 3    │ resembles another expected item's key           │ similarity     │
  │      │                                                 │ again          │
  └──────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────┴────────────────┘

  Step 2 made sense in v1, where keys were a fixed vocabulary. In v2 readers
  write free-text tags, so a ratio on tags is the word-match you rejected in the
  product, now sitting in the grader.

  What the behavior should be

  1. Sources first, exactly. An expected item is satisfied by a rendered item
     that cites one of its sources. When two expected items share a source
     (Sam's email backs both the pediatrician and the daycare item), each is
     satisfied only by a citation of a source unique to it. That's deterministic
     and needs no guard.
  2. When sources don't settle it, a decider decides. For the leftover cases (an
     expected item whose sources are all shared, or an item that cites nothing
     unique), ask Jev or the judge model: "does this rendered item cover this
     expected item?" with the item text and the key's title and notes. Same
     principle as #16 in the product: code narrows by hard facts, a model
     decides sameness.
  3. No ratio on keys anywhere. Topic keys stay useful for the merge-error
     diagnostic (how many items per expected item) and for history, not for
     credit.

  Cost: scorer only, no pipeline runs; about an hour to change, re-score both
  worlds for free (Jev questions cost fractions of a cent), refill the numbers
  in DESIGN.md. The numbers will move a little in both directions (some credits
  gained where tags drifted, some lost where a fuzzy key was flattering). I'd
  note in DESIGN.md that the scorer's matching was changed from fuzzy keys to
  exact sources plus a decider before the final scoring.

  Recommendation: do it. It removes the one place string similarity still
  decides anything in this repo, and it's the answer to "how do you know the
  digest is good?" that survives the walkthrough. Say go and I'll do it now; the
  alternative is to ship as is with #18 documented.

          eader sees the whole thread as written, the people on it (categ
          ory, tier, the profile's own words), facts code computed (busin
          ess days waiting, who wrote last) and what code retrieved becau
          se it might matter. It emits open-world **Findings**: what Aver
          y should do, why, priority, deadline, actions, ambiguity, contr
          adictions, each with verbatim citations that code checks exist.
           Nothing is limited to a schema of asks and commitments.
      11  - **Entities are the spine, code is the floor.** Code does the 
          math, enforces the hard rules (no drafts to Sam, recruiters onl
          y as a pattern, every claim cited, P0 only for a profile-tier c
          ontact, an incident or a family matter, suspicious content neve
          r P0) and runs eight **safety nets** under recall; a net a read
          er already covered attaches to that finding, the rest are logge
          d as rescues. "Is this the same thing?" is never string similar
          ity: TypeSafe's Jev (calibrated probabilities) answers first, t
          he LLM when Jev is unsure.
      12  - **Actions are a judgment.** Eleven action types; a draft only
           when Avery can finish it in under a minute. Honesty changes co
          nclusions: a stale inbox caps confidence and says "may be a syn
          c gap"; contradictions show both sides; drift becomes a propose
          d profile update, never an edit.
      13 -- **The eval is the product's mirror.** A 30-day world authored
         - as storylines whose `expectations:` are the answer key, review
         -ed by a human before any data existed. The target is digest-lev
         -el: P0 recall (the gate), trap assertions, noise, the one thing
         -. Stage metrics (reader recall on planted threads, sweep recall
         -, rescues, merge errors, contact classification) are diagnostic
         -s, each miss attributed to the first stage that lost it.       
      13 +- **The eval is the product's mirror.** A 30-day world authored
         + as storylines whose `expectations:` are the answer key, review
         +ed by a human before any data existed. The target is digest-lev
         +el: P0 recall (the gate), trap assertions, noise, the one thing
         +. Stage metrics (reader recall on planted threads, sweep recall
         +, rescues, merge errors, contact classification) are diagnostic
         +s, each miss attributed to the first stage that lost it. Credit
         + is decided by sources, never by string similarity.

  What changed in the grader (#23, closes #18)

  Before: Key resemblance (rapidfuzz ≥ 0.85) could credit or block an item
  Now: Exact key equality only
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Before: Citation match blocked when the item's key resembled another expected
  key
  Now: An item labeled exactly with another expected item's key is that item;
  otherwise a citation of a source unique to the expected item credits it
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Before: Token-in-entity heuristic in the #9 fallback
  Now: Source routes only (a labeled source, or the same storyline)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Before: —
  Now: Items citing only shared sources go to the product's decider (Jev, LLM
  when unsure), one question per expected item per day; every decision is
  listed in the report notes (dev 6, held-out 20)
